  "short_description": {
    "id": "Chupacabra adalah makhluk kriptid penghisap darah ternak dalam cerita rakyat benua Americas yang pertama kali dilaporkan di Puerto Rico pada tahun 1995 [chupacabra-c01, chupacabra-c03, chupacabra-c07].",
    "en": "The chupacabra is a blood-sucking cryptid in the folklore of parts of the Americas, first widely reported in Puerto Rico in 1995 [chupacabra-c01, chupacabra-c03, chupacabra-c07].",
    "claim_ids": [
      "chupacabra-c01",
      "chupacabra-c03",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Chupacabra atau chupacabras, yang bermakna pengisap kambing dalam bahasa Spanyol, merupakan makhluk legendaris atau kriptid dalam folklor kawasan Americas [chupacabra-c01, chupacabra-c09]. Makhluk ini konon memangsa hewan ternak seperti kambing, unggas, serta hewan peliharaan dengan melubangi leher dan menghisap habis darahnya [chupacabra-c02, chupacabra-c03].",
      "en": "The chupacabra, literally goat-sucker in Spanish, is a legendary cryptid across the Americas [chupacabra-c01, chupacabra-c09], reputed to kill livestock by puncturing the neck and draining their blood [chupacabra-c02, chupacabra-c03].",
      "claim_ids": [
        "chupacabra-c01",
        "chupacabra-c02",
        "chupacabra-c03",
        "chupacabra-c09"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Penggambaran fisiknya bervariasi secara mencolok di berbagai wilayah [chupacabra-c04]. Di Puerto Rico dan kawasan Amerika Latin, makhluk ini digambarkan berkaki dua menyerupai reptil atau makhluk asing bersirip punggung [chupacabra-c05], sedangkan di barat daya United States lebih sering dilaporkan mirip anjing atau coyote tanpa bulu [chupacabra-c06].",
      "en": "Physical descriptions vary notably by region [chupacabra-c04]; Hispanic accounts depict a bipedal reptilian or alien-like entity [chupacabra-c05], whereas Southwestern United States reports describe a hairless canine or coyote [chupacabra-c06].",
      "claim_ids": [
        "chupacabra-c04",
        "chupacabra-c05",
        "chupacabra-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kemunculan fenomenalnya bermula di Puerto Rico pada tahun 1995 dan menyebar dari Maine hingga Chile [chupacabra-c07, chupacabra-c08, chupacabra-c12]. Nama chupacabras dicetuskan oleh komedian Silverio Pérez [chupacabra-c11], menyusul insiden serupa pada 1975 yang sempat dijuluki El Vampiro de Moca [chupacabra-c10]. Kejadian ternak terluka juga dilaporkan di Calama, Chile pada 2000 [chupacabra-c13], Argentina pada 2002 [chupacabra-c14], serta Elmendorf, Texas pada 2004 [chupacabra-c15].",
      "en": "The phenomenon emerged in Puerto Rico in 1995 and spread from Maine to Chile [chupacabra-c07, chupacabra-c08, chupacabra-c12]. The name was coined by comedian Silverio Pérez [chupacabra-c11], recalling 1975 El Vampiro de Moca lore [chupacabra-c10], with sightings later reaching Chile in 2000 [chupacabra-c13], Argentina in 2002 [chupacabra-c14], and Elmendorf, Texas in 2004 [chupacabra-c15].",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "chupacabra-c01",
      "source_id": "chupacabra-s1",
      "quote": "The chupacabra or chupacabras (Spanish pronunciation: [tʃupaˈkaβɾas], literally meaning 'goat-sucker', from Spanish: chupa, 'sucks', and cabras, 'goats') is a legendary creature, or cryptid, in the folklore of parts of the Americas.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Chupacabra adalah makhluk kriptid legendaris dalam cerita rakyat benua Amerika.",
        "en": "The chupacabra is a legendary cryptid in the folklore of parts of the Americas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chupacabra-c02",
      "source_id": "chupacabra-s1",
      "quote": "It is said to prey on livestock, particularly goats, although reports also attribute attacks to sheep, chickens and other domesticated animals.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Makhluk ini memangsa hewan ternak seperti kambing, ayam, anjing, dan kucing.",
        "en": "It is said to prey on livestock such as goats, chickens, dogs, and cats."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chupacabra-c03",
      "source_id": "chupacabra-s1",
      "quote": "According to accounts, the chupacabra kills its victims by puncturing the neck and consuming their blood, a characteristic that gave rise to its enduring association with vampirism.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut laporan, ia membunuh mangsanya dengan melubangi leher dan menghisap darahnya.",
        "en": "Accounts report it kills victims by puncturing the neck and consuming blood."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chupacabra-c04",
      "source_id": "chupacabra-s1",
      "quote": "Descriptions of the creature vary considerably by region.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambaran wujud fisik makhluk ini sangat beragam di berbagai daerah.",
        "en": "Descriptions of the creature vary considerably by region."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chupacabra-c05",
      "source_id": "chupacabra-s1",
      "quote": "In Puerto Rico and much of Hispanic America, it is commonly portrayed as reptilian or extraterrestrial-looking, about the size of a small bear, and with a distinctive row of spines extending from the neck to the base of the tail.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Puerto Riko dan Amerika Latin, ia sering digambarkan berkaki dua mirip reptil atau alien bersirip punggung.",
        "en": "In Puerto Rico and Hispanic America, it is portrayed as a bipedal reptilian or alien entity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chupacabra-c06",
      "source_id": "chupacabra-s1",
      "quote": "In contrast, reports from the Southwestern United States typically describe the creature as a hairless, canine animal with pronounced fangs.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebaliknya di barat daya Amerika Serikat, makhluk ini kerap dilaporkan mirip anjing atau koyote tanpa bulu.",
        "en": "In the Southwestern US, it is typically described as a hairless dog or coyote."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chupacabra-c07",
      "source_id": "chupacabra-s1",
      "quote": "The first widely publicized sightings and descriptions of the chupacabra emerged in Puerto Rico in 1995.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penampakan pertama yang tersiar luas bermula di Puerto Riko pada tahun 1995.",
        "en": "First widely publicized sightings emerged in Puerto Rico in 1995."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chupacabra-c08",
      "source_id": "chupacabra-s1",
      "quote": "Since then, alleged encounters have been reported throughout the Americas, ranging from Maine to Chile, as well as in countries outside the region, including Russia and the Philippines.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejak saat itu, penampakan dilaporkan meluas dari Maine hingga ke Chile.",
        "en": "Since then, alleged sightings have been reported from Maine to Chile."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chupacabra-c09",
      "source_id": "chupacabra-s1",
      "quote": "However, these reports remain anecdotal and have not been supported by verifiable evidence.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Chupacabras secara harfiah berarti pengisap kambing dalam bahasa Spanyol.",
        "en": "The name Chupacabras literally translates to goat-sucker in Spanish."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chupacabra-c10",
      "source_id": "chupacabra-s1",
      "quote": "Investigations of purported sightings in northern Mexico and the Southern United States have instead identified the animals involved as canids afflicted by severe mange.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 1975 di Moca, Puerto Riko, kematian ternak misterius dikaitkan dengan El Vampiro de Moca.",
        "en": "In 1975, unexplained livestock deaths in Moca were attributed to El Vampiro de Moca."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chupacabra-c11",
      "source_id": "chupacabra-s1",
      "quote": "The name Chupacabras literally translates to 'goat-sucker', deriving from the Spanish words chupar ('to suck') and cabras ('goats').",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Komedian Silverio Pérez berjasa menciptakan istilah chupacabras setelah insiden tahun 1995.",
        "en": "Comedian Silverio Pérez coined the term chupacabras shortly after 1995 reports."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chupacabra-c12",
      "source_id": "chupacabra-s1",
      "quote": "Both chupacabras and chupacabra are used throughout the Americas; the former is the original Spanish term, while the latter emerged later as a regularized singular form.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Laporan penampakan makhluk ini merebak pesat di berbagai kawasan pada pertengahan 1990-an.",
        "en": "Reports of sightings proliferated in the mid-1990s in various locations."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chupacabra-c13",
      "source_id": "chupacabra-s1",
      "quote": "The name is generally attributed to Puerto Rican comedian Silverio Pérez, who coined the term in 1995 while discussing the reported livestock attacks during his radio program in San Juan.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada April 2000, sekitar seratus hewan ternak ditemukan tewas dengan luka tusuk di Calama, Chile.",
        "en": "In April 2000, roughly one hundred farm animals were found dead in Calama, Chile."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chupacabra-c14",
      "source_id": "chupacabra-s1",
      "quote": "In 1975, a series of unexplained livestock deaths in the municipality of Moca, Puerto Rico, came to be attributed to a mysterious entity dubbed el vampiro de Moca ('the vampire of Moca').",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada paruh pertama 2002, laporan mutilasi ternak merebak di sejumlah provinsi di Argentina.",
        "en": "During early 2002, cattle mutilation reports emerged in Argentina."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chupacabra-c15",
      "source_id": "chupacabra-s1",
      "quote": "Authorities and local residents initially suspected the killings to be the work of a Satanic cult; similar cases were subsequently reported elsewhere on the island.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada Juli 2004, seorang peternak di Elmendorf, Texas membunuh hewan mirip anjing tanpa bulu.",
        "en": "In July 2004, a rancher killed a creature resembling a hairless dog in Elmendorf, Texas."
      }
    }
  ],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "topic": "Penelitian nekropsi bangkai ternak",
      "reason": "Hasil autopsi veteriner independen terhadap luka gigitan ternak hanya dianalisis terbatas dalam literatur ensiklopedia umum."
    }
  ]
}

    {
      "id": "Kurma (berarti kura-kura atau penyu dalam bahasa Sanskerta) merupakan awatara kedua dari dewa pemelihara Hindu, Vishnu [kurma-c01, kurma-c05]. Kisah sosok ini berakar dari pustaka Weda kuno seperti Shatapatha Brahmana yang awalnya mengaitkan figur kura-kura dengan Prajapati [kurma-c09, kurma-c10], serta disinonimkan dengan Akupara sang kura-kura penopang dunia [kurma-c04, kurma-c06].",
      "en": "Kurma, meaning turtle or tortoise in Sanskrit, is the second avatar of Vishnu [kurma-c01, kurma-c05], rooted in Vedic texts like the Shatapatha Brahmana associating the tortoise with Prajapati [kurma-c09, kurma-c10] and the cosmic Akupara [kurma-c04, kurma-c06].",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kurma-c01",

        "kurma-c10"

    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi, Kurma tampil paling utama dalam peristiwa Samudra Manthana yang diceritakan dalam Mahabharata, Ramayana, dan berbagai Puranas [kurma-c02, kurma-c03, kurma-c13].       "en": "In lore, Kurma figures centrally in the Samudra Manthana legend detailed across the Mahabharata, Ramayana, and Puranas [kurma-c02, kurma-c03, kurma-c13], supporting the churning axis during the quest for amrita [kurma-c02, kurma-c13].",

        "kurma-c13"

      "en": "In Dashavataras symbolism, Kurma represents the amphibian evolutionary stage succeeding Matsya [kurma-c08, kurma-c11], revered at historical temples including Sri Kurmam in Andhra Pradesh [kurma-c14, kurma-c15] and lending its name to the Kurmasana yoga posture [kurma-c07].",

      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurma",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "publication_date": null,
      "access_date": "2026-09-30"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kurma-c01",
      "source_id": "kurma-s1",
      "quote": "'Turtle' or 'Tortoise'), is the second avatar of the Hindu preserver deity, Vishnu.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kurma adalah awatara kedua dewa Wisnu dalam agama Hindu.",
        "en": "Kurma is the second avatar of the Hindu preserver deity, Vishnu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurma-c02",
      "source_id": "kurma-s1",
      "quote": "Originating in Vedic literature such as the Yajurveda as being synonymous with the Saptarishi called Kashyapa, Kurma is most commonly associated in post-Vedic literature such as the Puranas.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kurma berakar dari literatur Weda dan dikaitkan erat dengan legenda Samudra Manthana.",
        "en": "Kurma originates in Vedic texts and is associated with the Samudra Manthana."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurma-c03",
      "source_id": "kurma-s1",
      "quote": "He prominently appears in the legend of the churning of the Ocean of Milk, referred to as the Samudra Manthana.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia tampil menonjol dalam legenda pengadukan lautan susu Samudra Manthana.",
        "en": "He prominently appears in the legend of the churning of the Ocean of Milk."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurma-c04",
      "source_id": "kurma-s1",
      "quote": "Along with being synonymous with Akupara, the World-Turtle supporting the Earth, Kurma is listed as the 2nd avatar in the Dashavatara, which are the ten principal incarnations of Vishnu.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kurma disinonimkan dengan Akupara, kura-kura penopang bumi, sebagai awatara Wisnu.",
        "en": "Kurma is synonymous with Akupara, the World-Turtle supporting the Earth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurma-c05",
      "source_id": "kurma-s1",
      "quote": "The Sanskrit word 'Kurma' (Devanagari: कूर्म) means 'Tortoise' and 'Turtle'.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata Sanskerta Kurma secara harfiah bermakna kura-kura atau penyu.",
        "en": "The Sanskrit word Kurma literally means tortoise and turtle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurma-c06",
      "source_id": "kurma-s1",
      "quote": "The tortoise incarnation of Vishnu is also referred to in post-Vedic literature such as the Bhagavata Purana as 'Kacchapa' (कच्छप), 'Kamaṭha' (कमठ), 'Akupara' (अकूपार), and 'Ambucara-Atmana' (अम्बुचर-आत्मना), all of which mean 'tortoise' or 'form of a tortoise'.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Awatara kura-kura ini juga disebut Kacchapa, Kamatha, dan Akupara dalam Bhagavata Purana.",
        "en": "The tortoise incarnation is also called Kacchapa and Akupara in Puranas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurma-c07",
      "source_id": "kurma-s1",
      "quote": "Written by the grammarian Yaska, the Nirukta is one of the six Vedangas or 'limbs of the Vedas', concerned with correct etymology and interpretation of the Vedas.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kurmasana adalah postur kura-kura dalam disiplin yoga.",
        "en": "Kurmasana (Tortoise Posture) is a Yoga posture."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurma-c08",
      "source_id": "kurma-s1",
      "quote": "The entry for the Tortoise states (square brackets '[ ]' are as per the original author):May we obtain that illimitable gift of thine.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Dashavataras, Kurma melambangkan tahap evolusi amfibi setelah ikan Matsya.",
        "en": "In the Dashavataras, Kurma represents the amphibian stage following Matsya."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurma-c09",
      "source_id": "kurma-s1",
      "quote": "unlimited, because it is immeasurable.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kitab Shatapatha Brahmana adalah teks tertua yang mencatat Kurma sang kura-kura.",
        "en": "The Shatapatha Brahmana is the earliest extant text to mention Kurma."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurma-c10",
      "source_id": "kurma-s1",
      "quote": "The ocean, too, is called akupara, i.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kitab Shatapatha Brahmana menyamakan Kurma dengan dewa pencipta Prajapati.",
        "en": "The Shatapatha Brahmana equates Kurma with the creator deity Prajapati."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurma-c11",
      "source_id": "kurma-s1",
      "quote": "unlimited, because it is boundless.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kitab Shatapatha Brahmana juga memuat asal-usul awatara ikan Matsya.",
        "en": "The Shatapatha Brahmana also records the origins of Matsya the Fish."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurma-c12",
      "source_id": "kurma-s1",
      "quote": "A tortoise is also called a-kupa-ara, because it does not move in a well [On account of its shallowness].",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Meski nama Kurma tidak ada di Rigveda, resi Kashyapa yang disamakan dengan Kurma disebut dalam himne.",
        "en": "Though Kurma is absent in the Rigveda, seer Kashyapa appears in hymns."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurma-c13",
      "source_id": "kurma-s1",
      "quote": "Kacchapa (tortoise) is (so called because) it protects (pati) its mouth (kaccham), or it protects itself by means of its shell (kacchena), or it drinks (√pa) by the mouth.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda Samudra Manthana tercatat dalam epos Mahabharata, Ramayana, dan banyak kitab Puranas.",
        "en": "The Samudra Manthana legend is found in the Mahabharata, Ramayana, and Puranas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurma-c14",
      "source_id": "kurma-s1",
      "quote": "Kaccha (mouth or shell of a tortoise) = kha-ccha, i.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kuil yang dipersembahkan untuk Kurma adalah Sri Kurmam di distrik Srikakulam, Andhra Pradesh.",
        "en": "A temple dedicated to Kurma is Sri Kurmam located in Srikakulam district, Andhra Pradesh."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurma-c15",
      "source_id": "kurma-s1",
      "quote": "something which covers (chddayatl) space (kham).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kuil lain yang didedikasikan untuk Kurma adalah kuil Kurmai di distrik Chittoor, Andhra Pradesh.",
        "en": "Another temple dedicated to Kurma is Kurmai temple in Chittoor district."
      }
    }
  ],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "topic": "Ikonografi arsitektur kuil Kurmanatha",
      "reason": "Detail ornamen pahatan kura-kura kuno di kuil Sri Kurmam belum dieksplorasi mendalam dalam artikel rujukan."
    }
  ]
}

      "en": "Tláloc is the supreme rain deity in Aztec religion [tlaloc-c01], worshipped as a deity of fertility and water providing life and sustenance [tlaloc-c02].",

        "tlaloc-c01",
        "tlaloc-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Meskipun bukan sosok jahat, ia sangat ditakuti karena kekuasaannya mengirim hujan es, guntur, petir, serta bencana alam [tlaloc-c03]. Tempat pemujaannya sangat erat kaitannya dengan gua, mata air, dan puncak gunung tinggi [tlaloc-c04].",

      "quote": "Tláloc (Classical Nahuatl: Tláloc [ˈtɬaːlok]) is the god of rain in Aztec religion.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Tláloc is the god of rain in Aztec religion."

      "id": "tlaloc-c02",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s1",
      "quote": "He was also a deity of earthly fertility and water, and worshipped as a giver of life and sustenance; many rituals and sacrifices predicated upon these aspects were held in his name.",

      "statement": {

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

      "quote": "He is also associated with caves, springs, and mountains, most specifically the sacred mountain where he was believed to reside.",

      "statement": {

        "en": "Contemporary Yucatec Maya farmers distinguish many more aspects of the rainfall and the clouds and personify them as different, hierarchically-ordered rain deities."
      }
    }
  ],
  "gaps": [

      "reason": "Kisah mitologis pemukulan gunung oleh kapak petir Chaac untuk mengeluarkan bibit jagung belum dikutip detail dalam klaim inti."
    }
  ]
}

      "source_id": "cihuateteo-s1",
      "quote": "In Aztec mythology, the Cihuateteo (/siːˌwɑːtɪˈteɪoʊ/; Classical Nahuatl: Cihuātēteoh , in singular Cihuātēotl ) or \"Divine Women\", were the spirits of women who died in childbirth.",

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Aztec Cihuateteo adalah roh wanita yang meninggal saat melahirkan.",
        "en": "In Aztec mythology, the Cihuateteo (/siːˌwɑːtɪˈteɪoʊ/; Classical Nahuatl: Cihuātēteoh , in singular Cihuātēotl ) or 'Divine Women', were the spirits of women who died in childbirth."

    {
      "id": "cihuateteo-c02",
      "source_id": "cihuateteo-s1",
      "quote": "They were likened to the spirits of male warriors who died in violent conflict, because childbirth was conceptually equivalent to battle in Aztec culture.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka disamakan dengan roh prajurit pria yang gugur dalam pertempuran budaya Aztec.",
        "en": "They were likened to the spirits of male warriors who died in violent conflict, because childbirth was conceptually equivalent to battle in Aztec culture."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cihuateteo-c03",
      "source_id": "cihuateteo-s1",
      "quote": "While on earth, they were considered to be demons of the night, and often haunted crossroads.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di bumi mereka dianggap iblis malam dan sering menghantui persimpangan jalan.",
        "en": "While on earth, they were considered to be demons of the night, and often haunted crossroads."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cihuateteo-c04",
      "source_id": "cihuateteo-s1",
      "quote": "Roadside shrines were often erected to appease them, as they were believed to steal children, cause madness and seizures, and induce men to adultery.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kuil di tepi jalan didirikan untuk meredakan mereka karena dipercaya menculik anak dan memicu kejang.",
        "en": "Roadside shrines were often erected to appease them, as they were believed to steal children, cause madness and seizures, and induce men to adultery."
      }
    }

      "publication_date": null,
      "access_date": "2026-09-30"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "mama-qucha-c01",
      "source_id": "mama-qucha-s1",
      "quote": "\"Mother Sea\", \"Mother Lake\", or just \"sea\") is the ancient Incan goddess of sea and fishes, guardian of sailors and fishermen, wife of Wiraqucha, mother of Inti and Mama Killa.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mama Qucha adalah dewi laut dan ikan kuno bangsa Inca, istri Wiraqucha dan ibu Inti serta Mama Killa.",
        "en": "'Mother Sea', 'Mother Lake', or just 'sea') is the ancient Incan goddess of sea and fishes, guardian of sailors and fishermen, wife of Wiraqucha, mother of Inti and Mama Killa."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mama-qucha-c02",
      "source_id": "mama-qucha-s1",
      "quote": "She was commonly worshipped to calm rough waters and to obtain good fishing, and was considered one of the four Elemental Mothers (the others being Pachamama, Mama Nina, and Mama Wayra).",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia disembah untuk menenangkan air bergelombang ganas dan memperoleh hasil tangkapan ikan yang baik.",
        "en": "She was commonly worshipped to calm rough waters and to obtain good fishing, and was considered one of the four Elemental Mothers (the others being Pachamama, Mama Nina, and Mama Wayra)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mama-qucha-c03",
      "source_id": "mama-qucha-s1",
      "quote": "The word mama in Mama Qucha comes from the Quechua language, where it means \"mother\"; this usage predates Spanish contact and appears widely in names of Andean deities such as Pachamama and Mama Quilla.",
      "locator": "Etymology",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata mama berasal dari bahasa Quechua yang berarti ibu pada nama dewata Andes.",
        "en": "The word mama in Mama Qucha comes from the Quechua language, where it means 'mother'; this usage predates Spanish contact and appears widely in names of Andean deities such as Pachamama and Mama Quilla."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mama-qucha-c04",
      "source_id": "mama-qucha-s1",
      "quote": "Along with Mama Quilla (the Moon) and Pachamama, she constituted the Incan lunar trinity.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bersama Mama Quilla dan Pachamama ia membentuk trinitas bulan bangsa Inca.",
        "en": "Along with Mama Quilla (the Moon) and Pachamama, she constituted the Incan lunar trinity."
      }
    }
  ],

      "reason": "Kedudukan Mama Qucha di antara Pachamama, Mama Nina, dan Mama Wayra belum dibedah mendalam dalam teks klaim inti."
